theorem exercise_4_21a {X : Type*} [MetricSpace X]
  (K F : Set X) (hK : IsCompact K) (hF : IsClosed F) (hKF : Disjoint K F) :
  ∃ (δ : ℝ), δ > 0 ∧ ∀ (p q : X), p ∈ K → q ∈ F → dist p q ≥ δ := by
  have hδ : ∃ δ > 0, ∀ p ∈ K, ∀ q ∈ F, dist p q ≥ δ := by
    exact?
  obtain ⟨δ, hδ_pos, hδ_sep⟩ := hδ
  exact ⟨δ, hδ_pos, fun p q hp hq => hδ_sep p hp q hq⟩

theorem exercise_4_6
  (f : ℝ → ℝ)
  (E : Set ℝ)
  (G : Set (ℝ × ℝ))
  (h₁ : IsCompact E)
  (h₂ : G = {(x, f x) | x ∈ E})
  : ContinuousOn f E ↔ IsCompact G := by
  constructor
  · intro h
    rw [h₂]
    -- We need to show that the set G is compact.
    -- Since E is compact and f is continuous on E, the set G = {(x, f(x)) | x ∈ E} is compact.
    exact isCompact_image (by continuity) h₁
  · intro h
    rw [h₂] at h
    -- We need to show that f is continuous on E.
    -- Since G is compact and E is compact, f must be continuous on E.
    exact h.of_isClosed_image (by continuity) isClosed_diagonal

theorem exercise_1_13a {f : ℂ → ℂ} (Ω : Set ℂ) (a b : Ω) (h : IsOpen Ω)
  (hf : DifferentiableOn ℂ f Ω) (hc : ∃ (c : ℝ), ∀ z ∈ Ω, (f z).re = c) :
  f a = f b := by
  obtain ⟨c, hc⟩ := hc
  have h1 : ∀ z ∈ Ω, (f z).re = c := hc
  have h2 : ∀ z ∈ Ω, (f z).im = 0 := by
    intro z hz
    have h3 : (f z).re = c := h1 z hz
    have h4 : (f z).im = 0 := by
      -- Use the fact that the imaginary part of f(z) is zero
      have h5 : f z = c := by
        have h6 : (f z).re = c := h1 z hz
        have h7 : (f z).im = 0 := by
          -- Since f(z) is real-valued, its imaginary part must be zero
          simp_all [Complex.ext_iff]

theorem exercise_3_22 (X : Type*) [MetricSpace X] [CompleteSpace X]
  (G : ℕ → Set X) (hG : ∀ n, IsOpen (G n) ∧ Dense (G n)) :
  ∃ x, ∀ n, x ∈ G n := by
  have h_dense : ∀ n, Dense (G n) := fun n => (hG n).2
  have h_open : ∀ n, IsOpen (G n) := fun n => (hG n).1
  -- Use the fact that each G n is dense to find a point in each G n
  have h_inter : ∀ n, (G n).Nonempty := fun n => by
    -- Since G n is dense, it is nonempty
    exact dense_iff_nonempty.mp (h_dense n)
  -- Use the fact that X is complete and the opens are nested to find a point in all G n
  choose x hx using fun n => h_inter n
  refine' ⟨x 0, _⟩
  intro n
  -- Use the fact that each G n is open and the sequence is Cauchy to find a common point
  exact Classical.by_contradiction fun h => by
    -- If x 0 is not in G n, then G n is disjoint from the ball around x 0
    have h' := hx n
    -- This leads to a contradiction because G n is dense
    exfalso
    exact h' (by
      -- Use the fact that G n is open and the sequence is Cauchy to find a common point
      have : x 0 ∈ G n := by
        -- Since G n is dense, it intersects every nonempty open set
        exact?
      simp_all)

theorem exercise_4_19
  {f : ℝ → ℝ} (hf : ∀ a b c, a < b → f a < c → c < f b → ∃ x, a < x ∧ x < b ∧ f x = c)
  (hg : ∀ r : ℚ, IsClosed {x | f x = r}) : Continuous f := by
  have h₀ : ∀ r : ℚ, IsClosed {x | f x = r} := hg
  have h₁ : Continuous f := by
    apply continuous_iff_continuousAt.2
    intro x
    apply tendsto_order.2 ⟨fun y hy => ?_, fun y hy => ?_⟩
    · -- Left limit
      rcases hf (x - 1) x (f x - 1) (by linarith) (by linarith [hy]) (by linarith [hy]) with ⟨z, hz₁, hz₂, hz₃⟩
      have h₂ : f z = f x - 1 := by linarith

        simp only [Set.mem_setOf_eq]
        linarith
      have h₄ : z < x := by linarith

      have h₃ : x < z := by linarith
      have h₄ : f x < f z := by linarith
      -- Use the properties of the function and the given conditions to show the limit
      exact?
  exact h₁

theorem exercise_2_24 {X : Type*} [MetricSpace X]
  (hX : ∀ (A : Set X), Infinite A → ∃ (x : X), x ∈ closure A) :
  SeparableSpace X := by
  have h1 : ∀ (A : Set X), Infinite A → ∃ (x : X), x ∈ closure A := hX
  have h2 : ∃ (s : Set X), s.Countable ∧ Dense s := by
    classical
    -- We will use the given property to construct a countable dense set
    let s := ⋃ (t : Set X) (ht : Set.Infinite t), {x | x ∈ closure t}
    -- We need to show that this set is countable and dense
    have h3 : s.Countable := by
      apply Set.countable_iUnion
      intro t
      apply Set.countable_iUnion
      intro ht
      exact (h1 t ht).choose_spec.1
    have h4 : Dense s := by
      -- We need to show that s is dense
      rw [dense_iff_closure_eq]
      -- The closure of s is the whole space
      apply?
    exact ⟨s, h3, h4⟩
  -- We have constructed a countable dense set, so the space is separable
  exact?

theorem exercise_1_14
  (z : ℂ) (h : abs z = 1)
  : (abs (1 + z)) ^ 2 + (abs (1 - z)) ^ 2 = 4 := by
  have h1 : abs (1 + z) = Complex.abs (1 + z) := rfl
  have h2 : abs (1 - z) = Complex.abs (1 - z) := rfl
  rw [h1, h2]
  norm_cast
  -- Use the property of complex numbers on the unit circle
  have h3 : Complex.abs z = 1 := h
  have h4 : Complex.abs (1 + z) = Complex.abs (1 + z) := rfl

  (f : α → β)
  (s : Set α)
  (h₁ : Continuous f)
  (h₂ : Dense s)
  : f '' Set.univ ⊆ closure (f '' s) :=

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

import Mathlib

open Topology Filter Real Complex TopologicalSpace Finset
open scoped BigOperators



--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PROOF SKETCH:
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
theorem exercise_4_4a
  {α : Type} [MetricSpace α]
  {β : Type} [MetricSpace β]
  (f : α → β)
  (s : Set α)
  (h₁ : Continuous f)
  (h₂ : Dense s)
  : f '' Set.univ ⊆ closure (f '' s) :=
```lean4
theorem exercise_4_4a
  {α : Type} [MetricSpace α]
  {β : Type} [MetricSpace β]
  (f : α → β)
  (s : Set α)
  (h₁ : Continuous f)
  (h₂ : Dense s)
  : f '' Set.univ ⊆ closure (f '' s) :=
by
  have h₃ : f '' Set.univ ⊆ closure (f '' s) := by
    -- For any point y in the image of f over the entire space
    rintro y ⟨x, hx, rfl⟩
    -- Since s is dense in α, for any x, there exists a sequence in s converging to x
    have : x ∈ closure s := h₂ x
    -- The image of a convergent sequence under a continuous function is a convergent sequence
    refine' mem_closure_image h₁.continuousAt this
  exact h₃

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PROOF:
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  : ‖x + y‖^2 + ‖x - y‖^2 = 2*‖x‖^2 + 2*‖y‖^2 := by sorry
  have h1 : ‖x + y‖^2 + ‖x - y‖^2 = 2*‖x‖^2 + 2*‖y‖^2 := by sorry

  <;> ring
  <;> simp_all [Real.norm_eq_abs]
  <;> linarith

  ∃ (f_lin : ℂ →L[ℂ] ℂ), ∀ x ∈ Ω, f x = f_lin x := by

      apply DifferentiableOn.sub hf

  have h₁ : f x = x := by
    have h₂ := hf
    have h₃ := h

    have h₅ : DifferentiableOn ℂ (fun w => f w - w) Ω := by

  : f = g := by

theorem exercise_1_19b (z : ℂ) (hz : abs z = 1) (s : ℕ → ℂ)
    (h : s = (λ n => ∑ i in (range n), i * z / i ^ 2)) :
    ∃ y, Tendsto s atTop (𝓝 y) := by
  -- We use the fact that the series converges to 0 when z = 1 and generalizes to |z| = 1
  use 0
  have h₁ : s = fun n => ∑ i in (range n), (i * z / i ^ 2) := h
  rw [h₁]
  apply tendsto_zero_iff_norm_tendsto_zero.mpr
  have h₂ : ∀ n : ℕ, ‖∑ i in (range n), (i * z / i ^ 2)‖ ≤ 0 := by
    intro n
    induction n with
    | zero => simp
    | succ n ih =>
      simp_all [Complex.norm_eq_abs, Complex.abs, Complex.normSq, range_succ, sum_range_succ, Nat.cast_add, Nat.cast_one, Nat.cast_mul, Nat.cast_pow]
      <;> cases z <;> simp_all [Complex.ext_iff, abs, normSq, pow_two, mul_add, mul_comm, mul_left_comm]
      <;> ring_nf
      <;> field_simp [hz]
      <;> nlinarith
  -- Using the fact that the norm of the sum is non-negative and the limit of the norm is 0, we conclude the limit of the sum is 0
  refine' squeeze_zero _ h₂
  exact fun n => by simp

  {X : Type*} [MetricSpace X] [CompleteSpace X]
  (E : ℕ → Set X)

  ∃ a, Set.iInter E = {a} := by
  have h1 : ∃ a : X, ∀ n : ℕ, a ∈ E n := by

          have h5 := hE'

theorem exercise_4_15 {f : ℝ → ℝ}
  (hf : Continuous f) (hof : IsOpenMap f) :
  Monotone f := by
  have h : Monotone f := by
    -- Use the fact that an open map and a continuous map can be used to prove monotonicity
    apply Monotone.of_isClosed_subset (isClosed_Ici) (isClosed_Ici)
    intro x
    -- For any x, if x ≤ y, then f(x) ≤ f(y)
    simp_all [IsOpenMap, Continuous, IsClosedMap]
    <;>
    exact?
  exact h

theorem exercise_1_19
  (n : ℕ)
  (a b c x : EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n))
  (r : ℝ)
  (h₁ : r > 0)
  (h₂ : 3 • c = 4 • b - a)
  (h₃ : 3 * r = 2 * ‖x - b‖)
  : ‖x - a‖ = 2 * ‖x - b‖ ↔ ‖x - c‖ = r := by
